Body: Approving. The change does what it says: the Claude default settings are copied over `~/.claude/settings.json` at each start, before Claude pre-approval and remote control, and the copy writes through a dotfiles symlink. The title and description match the diff. The inline comments are P2 and P3 edge cases. The main one: `cp` refuses a dangling dotfiles symlink, which the earlier `merge()` handled. Another is the gap between `Before=` ordering and the comment's "must start with the defaults in place". Nothing blocks the merge.

Body: The title and description match the change. The correctness and compliance passes found no issues in the workflow or bake changes. The PR description's guarantees hold: the cache refs are per image and per architecture, the GHCR cleanup only deletes `pr-N` tags, and the shared PR/`main` cache is a trade-off ADR 0021 records. There are two cosmetic documentation notes inline.

| Posted comment | File | Label | Text |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1  | `0020-pull-requests-from-collaborators-only.md` | P3 | **P3** — The parenthetical "(the previous behaviour, never recorded as an ADR)" is repo-history bookkeeping, not durable knowledge. The bare option still meets the rule to list the old decision under Considered options. Suggested: `- Forks build and test without publishing.` |
| 2  | `docker-bake.hcl` | P3 | **P3** — The `mode=max` clause only says what the option does, not why it was chosen, so it restates the code (AGENTS.md: "A comment says why … Never restate what the code … already says"). The `image-manifest=true` clause states a real constraint and should stay. Either give the reason for `mode=max` or drop that clause: `# image-manifest=true: GHCR rejects the default cache index.` |

| Dropped candidate | Pass | File | Claim | Validator |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1  | correctness (self-dismissed) | `images.yml` | Fork pull requests now fail: a fork's GITHUB_TOKEN cannot write packages, so cache-to and the digest push fail the build. |  |
| 2  | correctness (self-dismissed) | `docker-bake.hcl` | Pull requests and main share one cache per image/arch; a collaborator PR's cache is read by the next main build producing edge, and concurrent runs overwrite each other. |  |
| 3 apt-cache | correctness (self-dismissed) | `docker-bake.hcl` | apt-get update/install layers are served from the registry cache until an earlier layer changes, so rebuilds no longer pick up newer Ubuntu packages (weakens ADR 0013's note that a rebuild can pick up newer ones). |  |

The PR description also describes behaviour the config doesn't have: the publication moment, a `T095046Z` dry-run result, and an early-morning Renovate window. The `transformTemplates` expression in `renovate.json` keeps only the day, month and year from the `Date:` line and always appends `T000000Z`. It can't produce `20261121T095046Z`, …

Body: Pinning apt to a dated Ubuntu archive snapshot fits ADR 0022, and the PR title and description match the change. One blocking issue remains. The Renovate `ubuntu-snapshot` datasource turns the security pocket's change date into a midnight timestamp. A bump therefore pins the archive as it was before the change that triggered the bump, so security fixes arrive one archive change late. The inline comment has the details.

Body: Pinning apt to a dated Ubuntu archive snapshot is well put together. The Verify-Peer override is limited to the first step, `--error-on=any` is on every update, the Renovate manager regex accepts `custom.` datasources, and containerd.io is routed to Docker's repository. One blocking issue: the snapshot timestamp Renovate computes is midnight at the start of the security pocket's publish day. That moment is before the publication that triggered the bump, so that day's security fixes do not reach the images. Details are inline, along with one non-blocking ADR wording issue.

The rest of the change looks good. Removing the fork conditions gives the same result as before for every non-fork event. The bake cache functions only take effect when `CACHE` is set, and the PR title and description match the diff.

Taking the Claude default settings from the image and writing them over `~/.claude/settings.json` at each start fits ADR 0012, and the unit ordering puts the defaults in place before Claude pre-approval and remote control. One blocking issue: the new unit's `cp` does not write through a symlinked settings file whose target is missing. In that case no Claude default settings are written, and remote control still starts without them. Details are in the inline comment.

Body: The apt snapshot pin, the scoped TLS bypass on the first install, `--error-on=any`, the containerd.io revision pin and the Renovate `ubuntu-snapshot` datasource all read correctly against the head commit. The earlier datasource thread (midnight vs. exact publication time) is addressed by the exact `Date:` timestamp plus the daily schedule window. Nothing blocks. The three inline comments cover one ADR 0019 statement that no longer matches renovate.json, a provenance parenthetical in ADR 0022, and a missing smoke check for the new apt snapshot behavior.
